# Pedia Complete Project Code Review Questions

This file contains 420 project-specific, self-contained code review questions based on the current `PROJECT(9).md` implementation reference for Pedia. Each question intentionally repeats the required review discipline so it can be copied and given to an AI coding agent independently.

The questions use `PROJECT.md` as a map of expected contracts, but they repeatedly require verification against the real source code, project files, resources, manifests, and tests.

## 1. Repository authority, inventory, and documented scope

### Q001: Reconcile the complete repository inventory with the documented source ownership map

Inspect the repository from the solution root and build a complete inventory of production code, tests, XAML, resources, manifests, scripts, assets, generated files, and configuration files. Compare that inventory with the ownership map in `PROJECT.md`, especially the documented responsibilities of `Pedia.App`, `Pedia.Core`, and `Pedia.Tests`. Look for files that perform production behavior but are absent from the map, files whose actual responsibility differs materially from the description, duplicate implementations of the same concern, abandoned alternatives, or source files that are no longer compiled. Confirm project include/exclude rules and default SDK globbing before labeling a file unused. Report exact paths and explain whether any mismatch can mislead future review or change-impact analysis. Do not edit documentation merely because wording could be nicer; require a concrete factual mismatch.

Review discipline: Use `PROJECT.md` only as a map. Verify every conclusion against the current source, configuration, resources, and tests. AI reviewers can hallucinate or invent defects, so it is fully acceptable to conclude that this area is correct and needs no change. Do not force a finding, make speculative edits, or refactor working code for preference alone. If a real issue exists, first show concrete evidence, the reachable failure path, and the affected contract. Then make only the smallest clean fix that completely resolves it. Keep the code direct and easy to understand; less is better. Do not add unnecessary abstractions, helpers, dependencies, defensive branches, or broad rewrites. Preserve related behavior and invariants, and change or add only focused tests that genuinely protect the verified fix.

### Q002: Verify that current source code, not PROJECT.md or reference imagery, is treated as implementation authority

Trace several representative contracts from `PROJECT.md` into their actual implementation, including one UI behavior, one ViewModel flow, one repository transaction, one search behavior, and one backup or settings behavior. Confirm that no production decision is made by reading `PROJECT.md`, `README.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, or `pedia-ui-reference.png` at runtime. Check developer tooling or tests for assumptions that make the documentation or image a hidden source of truth. Identify any place where comments or tests merely restate the document without independently verifying behavior. Confirm that visual concepts present only in `pedia-ui-reference.png` have not accidentally been treated as required runtime columns, wording, or interaction contracts when XAML and ViewModels differ.

Review discipline: Use `PROJECT.md` only as a map. Verify every conclusion against the current source, configuration, resources, and tests. ### Q003: Find contradictions among PROJECT.md, README.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, manifests, and current implementation

Compare all repository documentation that describes architecture, supported features, non-goals, storage, packaging, privacy, and verification commands. For every material claim, determine whether current code and configuration still support it. Pay particular attention to local-only behavior, English-only resources, fixed dark theme, supported import/export formats, backup version, schema version, packaged and unpackaged profiles, Windows target versions, and the distinction between implemented behavior and runtime gates. Flag only contradictions that could cause an incorrect implementation, unsafe release decision, invalid test expectation, or misleading user/developer statement. Distinguish a stale document from a code defect and propose the smallest correction in the correct authority.

Review discipline: Use `PROJECT.md` only as a map. Verify every conclusion against the current source, configuration, resources, and tests. ### Q004: Audit untracked, ignored, generated, and build-output content for review blind spots

Inspect `.gitignore`, repository status, project output paths, generated XAML/C# artifacts, package output, local databases, logs, settings, temporary backup files, and test temp directories. Determine whether important source, migration SQL, resources, assets, signing configuration, or test data could be excluded from normal review or accidentally generated only on one machine. Also check whether user data, logs, backup archives, publish output, symbols, certificates, or secrets could be committed. Verify that a clean clone can regenerate all ignored outputs from checked-in inputs. Do not classify standard `bin`, `obj`, or package caches as a problem unless configuration makes them authoritative or leaks sensitive content.

### Q005: Check for dormant production code and alternate implementations that bypass the documented architecture

Search the entire solution for unused services, repositories, provider abstractions, windows, pages, commands, feature flags, build symbols, experimental branches, duplicate serializers, alternate database paths, or old UI controls. Confirm through references, DI registration, XAML instantiation, reflection, source generators, and project inclusion whether each suspect is truly unreachable. Pay special attention to any latent HTTP, AI, cloud, synchronization, WebView, telemetry, update-checking, or external-content implementation that would weaken the explicit non-goals even if no visible UI exposes it. Remove code only when reachability and lack of intended use are established; do not delete extension points solely because they are currently unused if a concrete contract or test requires them.

### Q006: Verify that explicit product non-goals are not contradicted by packages, capabilities, registrations, or hidden execution paths

Audit package references, transitive dependencies, manifests, app startup, DI registrations, background tasks, timers, URI handlers, WebView controls, network APIs, environment reads, and platform capabilities for evidence of Wikipedia retrieval, HTTP download, scraping, cloud sync, accounts, telemetry, billing, AI, vector search, automatic classification, background refresh, file watching, topic drag-and-drop, or article-body autosave. Distinguish a harmless transitive API from a reachable feature. Confirm stored source URLs remain inert metadata until an explicit user action. If any capability exists only for Windows App SDK requirements, document that evidence rather than treating the name alone as a violation.

### Q007: Confirm that no source file silently broadens Pedia beyond its local private-library product definition

Review user-visible commands, startup behavior, services, scheduled work, file-system watchers, URI activation, clipboard use, and external process launch. Determine whether any code performs work outside the stated local knowledge-library model without an explicit user action or clear UI disclosure. Check especially automatic folder scans, automatic imported-file refresh, hidden network probes, update checks, shell integration, protocol registration, or background indexing beyond the local database. Verify that import reads only selected files, export and backup write only selected destinations, and source opening uses the Windows default handler only after validation and user initiation.

### Q008: Review repository naming and namespace consistency for misleading ownership or accidental coupling

Inspect project names, namespaces, folder placement, type names, assembly names, root namespaces, XAML class names, resource URIs, test-linked source paths, and package identity. Do not rename stable public or persisted identifiers for cosmetic uniformity; require a concrete maintenance, correctness, serialization, XAML activation, or dependency-direction problem.

### Q009: Audit repository-level legal and third-party attribution inputs without inventing release requirements

Inspect package metadata, included fonts or images, app assets, About text, third-party notices, licenses, and any copied sample content. Determine whether the checked-in materials have identifiable origin and whether current user-visible attribution claims are supported by repository evidence. Verify that package licenses are not confused with a requirement to reproduce every license in the UI, and distinguish legal uncertainty from a code defect. Focus on concrete missing files, incorrect attribution strings, incompatible embedded assets, or sample content whose source/license handling contradicts the product's local-data and source-metadata model.

### Q010: Check that the repository can be reviewed and built without undocumented machine-specific state

Starting from the documented root commands, identify every required SDK, workload, platform component, environment variable, certificate, local path, launch profile, test architecture setting, and Windows runtime dependency. Inspect project files and scripts for absolute paths, user-specific directories, implicit Visual Studio state, hidden NuGet feeds, locally installed assets, or generated files not represented in source control. Confirm which requirements are genuine build prerequisites and which are runtime gates. A missing production signing certificate may be an honest release limitation rather than a source defect; classify it accurately.

## 2. Solution, build, target-framework, package, and compiler configuration

### Q011: Validate every solution configuration and platform mapping, especially forced x64 behavior

Inspect `Pedia.sln`, all project files, solution configuration sections, project configuration mappings, RuntimeIdentifiers, PlatformTarget values, and launch settings. Verify that Debug and Release builds selected as Any CPU or x86 cannot accidentally produce an incompatible App architecture when the documented contract says `Pedia.App` maps to x64. Confirm `Pedia.Core` and `Pedia.Tests` resolve compatible architectures and that test discovery does not silently skip because of a host mismatch. Exercise or statically prove each meaningful solution configuration rather than checking only the default IDE selection. Flag duplicate or contradictory platform properties only when they can change output or confuse a clean build.

Review discipline: Use `PROJECT.md` only as a map. Verify every conclusion against the current source, configuration, resources, and tests. ### Q012: Review shared compiler settings and project-level overrides for inconsistent semantics

Inspect `Directory.Build.props` and every `.csproj` for nullable context, implicit usings, language version, deterministic build, warning configuration, unsafe code, checked arithmetic, optimization, debug symbols, generated documentation, and any project-specific override. Confirm the effective values for App, Core, and Tests rather than assuming central properties always win. Search for `#nullable`, warning suppressions, pragmas, and conditional property groups that create different correctness guarantees across files or configurations. Determine whether `LangVersion=latest` is intentional and compatible with the pinned SDK/build environment. Do not recommend enabling every possible compiler option without a demonstrated benefit or current inconsistency.

Review discipline: Use `PROJECT.md` only as a map. Verify every conclusion against the current source, configuration, resources, and tests. ### Q013: Verify central package management is the single effective version authority

Inspect `Directory.Packages.props`, all `PackageReference` items, transitive package declarations, test-only dependencies, and conditional references. Confirm direct package references do not carry conflicting inline versions and that central package management is actually enabled and applied. Look for duplicate packages, packages referenced by the wrong project, production assemblies pulling test dependencies, or an unused direct dependency that expands deployment or attack surface. Check whether version overrides are deliberate and documented by build behavior. Do not remove a package based only on an IDE's superficial unused indication; verify compile, XAML, source-generator, runtime, and packaging use.

### Q017: Validate the self-contained win-x64 publish profile end to end at the configuration level

### Q018: Inspect build reproducibility and dependency-restore determinism

Determine whether two clean restores can resolve materially different direct dependencies or source inputs. Check whether absence of a lock file is a real reproducibility problem in this repository or simply an accepted central-version model. Look for hidden local feeds, package source mapping issues, or conditional references dependent on machine state. Recommend only the smallest change needed for a verified nondeterministic input.

### Q019: Audit warning suppressions, analyzer configuration, and zero-warning claims

Inspect `.editorconfig`, MSBuild warning properties, `NoWarn`, `WarningsAsErrors`, pragmas, generated-code exclusions, nullable suppressions, and analyzer package configuration. Re-run or inspect both documented Release build and test output where possible. Determine whether the reported zero warnings is genuine or achieved by broad suppression that hides actionable compiler, platform-compatibility, trimming, XAML, or nullable problems. For every suppression, confirm scope and rationale. Do not turn every informational analyzer into an error or introduce a large analyzer suite unless a concrete gap justifies it.

## 3. Architecture, dependency direction, composition, and service boundaries

### Q026: Verify that App.ConfigureServices is the sole composition root

Search for every `ServiceCollection`, `BuildServiceProvider`, direct singleton instance, static service accessor, and manual ViewModel or repository construction in production code. Confirm `App.ConfigureServices` is the only composition root and that tests construct isolated graphs intentionally. Look for nested service providers, service locator patterns, or XAML-created objects that bypass required dependencies and lifetime validation. Trace `MainWindow` resolution and activation from launch. If an object is intentionally constructed per operation, such as per-import composition, confirm it does not create a second hidden application container.

### Q420: Perform a final invariant-driven build and regression verification

Recheck the fourteen key review invariants: topic deletion preserves articles; primary topics; atomic graph/search save; no Trash FTS; complete bulk validation; no SQL structure injection; stale async rejection; universal dirty guards; one writer lease; validated restore/safety path; no implicit export/backup overwrite; atomic normalized settings; edited samples become user content; and offline/non-AI boundaries. Report exact commands/results and any runtime gates still untested. Do not claim success if a command was not actually run.
